# Known issues (alpha.7 line, re-checked on 2026-11-19; first written for 0.1.0-alpha.1)

Open: BUG 1, UNPROVEN 11, LIMIT 5 (this part of the file: the header, "Do not use it for" and "Hardware: what was tested"; the counts of the whole file are recomputed by the checker at the end)

This is the honest list. It comes from the "Not verified" and "Known gaps" sections of the README, the "Unverified" notes in `docs/`, and every row of
[FEATURES.md](FEATURES.md) that is not `done` (the full generated list is the appendix at the end). If something is not here and it does not work, please
report it: see [SUPPORT.md](SUPPORT.md).

Every entry has one class: **BUG** (a real defect with a symptom you can reproduce; the plan is in the entry), **UNPROVEN** (written and tested with fakes or
in QEMU, never tried on the real machine or service; it closes by a named hardware session or a row of
[docs/development/TEST-PROOF-QUEUE.md](docs/development/TEST-PROOF-QUEUE.md), not by more code) or **LIMIT** (an intended limit or a design decision).
Fixed entries are deleted and listed in [CHANGELOG.md](CHANGELOG.md).

**The state in one paragraph (2026-11-19):** IYAKKI has been run in QEMU, in a desktop window, in containers and on real computers. Real machines that booted
an IYAKKI image: a Mac mini 2018 (Macmini8,1, T2; since 2026-10-18), a 2020 MacBook Pro (MacBookPro16,2), a MacBook Pro 14,1, an HP Pavilion 16 (Intel + NVIDIA), a
Lenovo Legion Pro 5 and one AMD PC (nothing else about that one was written down). The alpha.6-line image of 2026-11-18 ran read-only hardware checks on the Lenovo
and the Mac mini 2018 (results in the "Hardware run 2026-11-18" section of [docs/development/TEST-PROOF-QUEUE.md](docs/development/TEST-PROOF-QUEUE.md); evidence kept outside the repository).
The alpha.7 candidate image (signed, lean) boots in QEMU; it has not been put on a real machine yet. On an Apple Silicon Mac (M6) the VM recipe (F-APPLE-02)
brought up k3s Ready on 2026-11-19. What worked on each machine beyond booting is recorded only where an entry below or a row of [FEATURES.md](FEATURES.md) says
so. It has not been run on any other PC or Mac, a managed cloud cluster or against most vendors' live services. The table is in
[docs/TESTED-HARDWARE.md](docs/TESTED-HARDWARE.md) and [docs/HARDWARE.md](docs/HARDWARE.md). **This alpha is a preview for developers and testers.**

## Do not use it for

* KI-001 [LIMIT] Production, or any machine that holds data you cannot lose: the installer (`iyakki-usb --install`, `iyakki install`) erases the whole drive you choose, and the data volume is not encrypted (vaults and the optional encrypted home folder, one per account, are) | Closes by: a data-volume encryption feature and a non-destructive install path, neither planned for alpha | Row: none
* KI-002 [LIMIT] Production clusters: the deploy tools refuse profiles that look like production, and the cluster side ran only against fake `kubectl` runners and local k3s (including the dev cluster on the Mac mini), never a managed (EKS/GKE) or real remote cluster | Closes by: a decision to support managed clusters, then a session on one | Row: none
* KI-003 [UNPROVEN] Secure Boot on real hardware: shim + MOK ([docs/SECURE-BOOT.md](docs/SECURE-BOOT.md)) is proven in QEMU with OVMF only (signed GRUB, kernel and modules boot, unsigned ones are refused; 17 checks, and the alpha.7 signed lean image boots in QEMU); no real PC or T2 Mac result is recorded. Images are signed only when built with `IYAKKI_SB_KEY`/`IYAKKI_SB_CERT`, the initial RAM disk is not signed, GRUB 2.12 lacks the 2025 security fixes, and driver packs with unsigned modules are refused under Secure Boot | Closes by: a hardware session that enrols the owner's key (MOK) and boots the signed image on a real PC and a T2 Mac | Row: none
* KI-004 [UNPROVEN] The private-store registry on the dev cluster is closed to the network in code (`localreg_tests::the_registry_manifest_has_no_network_exposure`, `localreg_tests::an_existing_nodeport_registry_is_converted_to_loopback_and_audited`, `regsetup::tests::applying_over_an_open_nodeport_registry_closes_it_first`, `k3sdev_tests::a_cluster_that_becomes_ready_closes_an_old_open_registry_and_audits_it`; all four exist in the tree), but nobody has checked it on a running image, and the browser front end's self-made HTTPS certificate has had no outside review, so nothing here should face the internet | Closes by: on an image with the dev cluster and the private-store registry on, `curl http://<ip>:30500/` from another computer is refused (connection refused); the outside review is a separate, unplanned job | Row: none

The isolation of Linux windows on an image is a chroot over a covered mount, not a replaced root (see [docs/LINUX-APPS.md](docs/LINUX-APPS.md)); the failed install on the Mac is KI-075.

## Hardware: what was tested

Short version; the maintained table is [docs/TESTED-HARDWARE.md](docs/TESTED-HARDWARE.md), the per-capability table is
[docs/project/HARDWARE-MATRIX.md](docs/project/HARDWARE-MATRIX.md) and how to report a machine is [docs/BOOT-TEST.md](docs/BOOT-TEST.md) (`iyakki hwreport`). (F-BOOT-01)

* **Mac mini 2018 (Macmini8,1, T2; Intel UHD 630; 32 GB), the main test machine.** First booted from a USB SSD (Samsung T7) on 2026-10-18/19, later from its internal
  disk: GPT stick written by `iyakki-usb`, GRUB on UEFI, data volume and persistent `/home`, HDMI audio and the built-in speaker and jack (out-of-tree `apple-bce`), software
  volume, USB webcam, the k3s dev cluster, Claude Code and Copilot CLI sign-in, Lemonade on the LAN and the full web engine (Google, YouTube with VP9). Details:
  ALPHA-GATE.md items 46 to 54. On 2026-11-18 the alpha.6-line image ran the read-only hardware recipes there (T2 audio card, 4K link, k3s, Wi-Fi up with an address).
* **MacBook Pro 2020 with the T2 chip (MacBookPro16,2), owner report.** USB SSD and internal disk (first internal install 2026-10-24): keyboard, trackpad, speaker sound
  (`plughw`), the GPU browser helper, YouTube, Touch Bar backlight, k3s Ready, internal panel (2560x1600) and a DisplayPort monitor, Wi-Fi with Apple's firmware, the built-in camera,
  developer mode over Wi-Fi. Open there: the IYAKKI-drawn Touch Bar strip, the built-in microphone (off on purpose, capture hung the machine), upgrading that install, battery, sleep (KI-013 and the rows named in the matrix).
* **MacBook Pro 14,1 (2017, no T2), HP Pavilion 16-af0 (Intel + NVIDIA; needed the x2APIC kernel options) and Lenovo laptops:** the final alpha.2 image booted on all of them (owner, 2026-11-10), and the 2026-11-09 image
  ran well on the Mac mini 2018, the MacBook Pro, the HP and the Lenovo (owner, 2026-11-09). Bluetooth pairing, phone sound and tether were verified on the MacBook Pro 14,1 (2026-11-08/10); the HP camera worked. The Lenovo Legion Pro 5 also ran the read-only recipes on 2026-11-18.
* **Raspberry Pi 5 (16 GB, Model B Rev 1.1), first run 2026-11-24 (owner):** booted to the IYAKKI home screen on HDMI with USB keyboard and mouse, after the D0 device tree was put on the card (KI-1030). The userland on that card was old (KI-1034). Ethernet, sound, restart and the alpha.10 program were not tried yet.
* **AMD Kria KV260 Starter Kit, first run 2026-11-24 (owner):** U-Boot found the menu on the card, Linux 6.12.40 booted, IYAKKI reached its shell on DisplayPort (1920x1080) with the card's device tree for carrier rev B (KI-1031), USB keyboard, mouse and Gigabit Ethernet worked. Developer mode pairs but signed requests are refused (KI-1033). Shutdown, a second start, sound and camera were not tried.
* **One AMD PC, owner report only:** it booted; model, GPU and what else worked are not recorded.
* **Expected to work, untested:** other Intel PCs and laptops (UEFI or BIOS), Intel Macs without a T2 chip, the Mac mini 2014, other cameras, sound devices and drives. NVIDIA cards run on `nouveau` (Turing and newer) unless the person installs the vendor driver ([docs/GPU-K3S.md](docs/GPU-K3S.md)).

**Open items on real hardware:**

* KI-005 [UNPROVEN] Wi-Fi on the Mac mini 2018 (Broadcom BCM4364) needs Apple's firmware, which IYAKKI never ships: Settings, Mac drivers installs it at the person's request (Download from Apple, or a folder, stick or disk image; `docs/MAC-WIFI-BLUETOOTH-SETUP.md`). The 2020 T2 MacBook Pro got Wi-Fi this way (2026-10-26/27) and the Mac mini 2018 showed `wlan0` with an address on 2026-11-18, but the install from a clean state (Download from Apple, then association) has not been recorded there; the Mac mini 2014's BCM4360 needs Broadcom's non-free `wl` built by the person (`dist/drivers/build-wl-pack.sh`), never loaded on a real Mac (KI-094) | Closes by: a hardware session on a Mac mini 2018 with no firmware installed: run Mac drivers, Download from Apple, and join a network | Row: F-NET-02
* KI-006 [LIMIT] Bluetooth is limited on the Mac mini 2018: its BCM4364 has no firmware file in any macOS version Apple offers and there is no legal source, so the controller runs in ROM state (registers as `hci0`, no valid address; the alpha.6 hardware recipe on 2026-11-18 confirmed it again). Settings, Bluetooth shows the state and scans for 10 seconds; the drivers stay set aside until the person chooses "Load the Bluetooth driver anyway". IYAKKI's own stack (passkey or PIN pairing, keyboards and mice, phone sound, tether) worked on a MacBook Pro 14,1; pairing on the Mac mini 2018 is untried, as are low-energy devices and a real PIN device anywhere. Use a USB Bluetooth adapter | Closes by: not closable on this model (no legal firmware source); untried parts: pair a device through a USB adapter, a BLE device and a PIN device | Row: F-NET-11
* KI-007 [UNPROVEN] The fix for a 5.3 s input-device scan stall at boot and the shared sound mixer fix (YouTube had no sound while the radio played) are in the images since alpha.2 and those images run on the Mac mini 2018 and three other computers, but neither fix was measured on its own | Closes by: a hardware session: read the boot log timing of the input scan and play YouTube while the radio plays | Row: F-SND-06
* KI-008 [UNPROVEN] The boot is slow at the start on a Mac mini (images of 2026-10: 20 to 30 s at the logo reading the 160 MB initramfs through the Mac firmware's USB driver, first shell picture about 9 s after the kernel starts); the own boot loader is the default since 2026-11-07 ([docs/BOOT-LOADER.md](docs/BOOT-LOADER.md)) but nobody has timed the wait with it | Closes by: a hardware session: power on a Mac mini 2018 from the current T7 stick and time power-on to the first IYAKKI picture and to the sign-in screen | Row: F-BOOT-20
* KI-009 [UNPROVEN] Only the 2020 T2 MacBook Pro (MacBookPro16,2) and the 2017 MacBookPro14,1 booted; the 2019 model and the other T2 MacBooks (2018 to 2020) were not tried | Closes by: a hardware report (`iyakki hwreport`) from each model | Row: F-BOOT-01
* KI-010 [LIMIT] Apple Silicon: bare metal is out of scope and not promised (owner decision 2026-10-27; F-BOOT-09, F-DRIVERS-12 and F-DRIVERS-19 are `deprecated`); the way is a light VM with k3s and a bridge to the model runner ([docs/APPLE-SILICON.md](docs/APPLE-SILICON.md)). The VM recipe ran on an Apple Silicon Mac (M6, Lima 2.3.0, vz) on 2026-11-19 and the node was Ready (the older failure is KI-1015); not yet tried there: the k3s API over the loopback forward and a pod | Closes by: a decision to support bare metal (none planned); the VM part closes by F-APPLE-02 passing the forward and a pod on the M6 | Row: F-APPLE-02
* KI-011 [UNPROVEN] ARM64: the image boots in QEMU and, since 2026-11-24, on two real boards (a Raspberry Pi 5 and an AMD Kria KV260, KI-1030 to KI-1035 for what had to be worked around); no ARM64 cluster or CLI install was tried | Closes by: the owner's checklists F-RPI-08 and F-KV260-01 answered on the boards | Row: F-BOOT-08
* KI-012 [UNPROVEN] Bare-metal PCs: the HP Pavilion 16 and the Lenovo Legion Pro 5 boot the images and the Lenovo ran the read-only recipes on 2026-11-18 (the problems found are KI-080 to KI-082 and KI-1013); touchpad, sound, Wi-Fi and GPU on the HP are not recorded, and all other PCs are untested. GPUs other than the Mac mini's Intel UHD 630 and the MacBook's integrated GPU were never recorded as tried | Closes by: a hardware session: run CHECK-UP ([docs/CHECKUP.md](docs/CHECKUP.md)) on the HP and save the result file; each pass or fail closes a cell of `docs/project/HARDWARE-MATRIX.md` | Row: F-CHECKUP-01
* KI-013 [UNPROVEN] Microphones: on the T2 MacBook Pro an external USB microphone and the built-in camera (640x480 YUYV) work; the built-in microphone of T2 Macs stays off on purpose (capture hung the machine). The browser microphone path (`libgstiyakkimic.so`, lists capture streams and opens them through `plughw`; the Voice tile's chooser) was written and tested with fake devices and a PC sound card, never run on the Mac, and whether `Digital Mic` delivers sound there is unproven (needs the `apple_bce` capture switch and the media-stream web engine rebuild, F-BROW-72) | Closes by: a hardware session on the T2 MacBook Pro: a page records through the Browser's microphone chooser and the `iyakki-mic:` log lines are read | Row: F-VOICE-04
* KI-014 [UNPROVEN] The `hw` image (firmware blobs) was never booted on a real machine | Closes by: a hardware session booting the `hw` image on a PC that needs firmware | Row: F-FW-07
* KI-015 [BUG] Text that does not fit: the overflow detector (`monkey_tests`) still lists the System tile's sound row at 1366x768 (it can carry a connected monitor's own name from the audio card, found 2026-11-15) and, in the full size matrix at 360x740, the Skills hint line (85%, 100%), Media's Shuffle and Repeat (125%, 150%), the Radio volume bars (125%), the Scan caption (150%) and Calendar's Alarms button (150%), all in `KNOWN_OVERFLOWS` and `KNOWN_OVERFLOWS_MATRIX`, re-checked case by case on 2026-11-19; at 360 wide most Settings pages also keep long unwrapped explanation lines because the page list takes the width, and very large UI sizes on small screens overflow many pages. Bare metal draws through the fbdev framebuffer (software rendered, single thread), so web video decodes in software (a 720p YouTube video dropped about 57 of 628 frames, 1080p60 drops more); the detector is a heuristic never compared with a real screen and sizes under 360 wide are out of scope. The old page-by-page table of 2026-10-28 is dropped as stale: regenerate it with `IYAKKI_OVERFLOW_REPORT=1 cargo test -p iyakki-shell --lib overflow_every -- --nocapture` | Closes by: wrap or shorten each listed text (shell tile code), remove its line from the known list and keep the test green; the web-video speed closes only with a KMS/GPU path, not planned for alpha | Row: F-QA-15
* KI-016 [LIMIT] Boot time and size: about 1.6 s to the sign-in screen in QEMU (the Mac mini takes much longer, see KI-008); the old spec targets of 30 MB and 100 ms are not met (the libre ISO is about 60 MB, the web image about 2 GB) and the figures are from 2026-10, not re-measured | Closes by: the lean image and size budgets (`dist/release/check-budgets.py`) decide the real targets; not a goal for alpha | Row: none
* KI-160 [UNPROVEN] First USB-C plug of a 4K monitor listed only 1080p (Mac mini 2018, kernel 6.12, i915, 2026-11-12): connector `DP-1` was connected with a valid EDID (3840x2160 at 60 Hz preferred) but its `modes` held only the generic fallback set; after another cable was plugged and removed the same cable gave 3840x2160. Cause not proven. Recovery written (F-DISP-13): a connected display whose mode list lacks the EDID's preferred size is asked again by writing `detect` to its `status` file at boot, on every display event and on Settings, Display, Look again (chat tool `displays_look_again`), three tries 1, 3 and 8 s apart, one `iyakki.display_retry` log line each, tested with fake sysfs folders only | Closes by: a hardware session on the Mac mini 2018 with the 4K monitor: unplug, boot with the monitor unplugged, plug it in (the checklist is in `docs/development/MANUAL-TEST-CHECKLIST-2026-11-14.md`) | Row: F-DISP-13

* KI-1030 [BUG] Raspberry Pi 5: the image carries only the C0 device tree (`bcm2712-rpi-5-b.dtb`). A board with the D0 SoC stepping (the 16 GB model, Model B Rev 1.1) panics with an SError in `bcm2712_pull_config_set` (through `pinctrl_bind_pins`) about 1.5 to 2.7 s after the kernel starts, on whatever device sets a pin pull first; the `iyakki.board` boot then repeats because of `panic=10`. Workaround used on 2026-11-24: build `bcm2712d0-rpi-5-b.dtb` from the pinned Raspberry Pi tree and set `device_tree=` to it in `config.txt` (the board then booted). Whether the Pi firmware picks the right tree by itself when `device_tree=` is left out is not tried | Closes by: both trees in the image and the generated `config.txt`, verified on a C0 and a D0 board | Row: F-RPI-04 (also F-RPI-08)
* KI-1031 [BUG] AMD Kria KV260: the default menu entry (no `fdt` line, the board's own device tree) gives no display: the kernel logs `zynqmp-dpsub: missing ports, update DT bindings`, registers `fb0`, then fails the probe with `-22` and IYAKKI runs headless. The entry `IYAKKI (device tree from the SD card)` (the kernel's own tree for carrier rev B) shows the shell on DisplayPort at 1920x1080. Workaround: make that entry the `default` in `extlinux.conf` (rev A carrier cards: the rev A entry) Update 2026-11-25: on the loader route (the board's U-Boot starts the IYAKKI loader) the entries now carry an `fdt` line for the rev B tree on the card and the display probed (`zynqmp-dpsub` 1920x1080, seen on the serial log; the screen itself was not looked at) | Closes by: the image's default entry, or a `fdtdir` choice by board, verified on a rev A and a rev B carrier | Row: F-KV260-01 (also F-KV260-04)
* KI-1032 [BUG] KV260 SD image: the data partition in the template is not empty. It holds a TLS key and certificate, a device id, an audit log and user records from the build-time QEMU test, so every card written from that image would share one TLS key (found 2026-11-24; nothing was published from that image) | Closes by: a template with an empty data partition (the first start formats it, as the Pi image does), and the same check for every image | Row: F-KV260-01 (also F-KV260-02)
* KI-1033 [UNPROVEN] Developer mode on the KV260 and the Pi 5: pairing with a code works (the Pi's audit log shows the key registered), but every later signed request (`dev info`, `dev whoami`) answers "the request's signature is not valid", with the alpha.8-dev and alpha.10-dev program on the board. Ruled out on 2026-11-24: the clock (set from a time server), the certificate fingerprint, the signing text (unchanged since alpha.7), the client version, the trust switch, the key (this computer's key starts `04c7 aefa ffa0 10c9`, as registered), and the build: the request signing tests (`devmode::auth`) and all five developer-mode integration tests (`iyakki-remote/tests/devmode.rs`: pairing, signed requests, a revoked key, over real TLS sockets) pass on the real Pi 5, built both in the dev and in the device profile, and the signing tests pass on an aarch64 Mac. What differs is the real request from this x86 computer to the board's running program | Closes by: running the client on the Pi against its own server (needs a pairing code opened on the Pi), or capturing one refused request | Row: F-KV260-04
* KI-1034 [BUG] Raspberry Pi 5 card: the userland on the card was the old aarch64 root (release file `0.1.0-alpha.1`, 2026-10-18, kernel 6.12.112) because `make-pi-image.sh` was not given a fresh program and the root's `iyakki-release` was not written for the board | Closes by: the image build passing `--iyakki` and writing its own release file (the alpha.10 program was swapped in by hand for the 2026-11-24 test) | Row: F-RPI-04 (also F-RPI-08)
* KI-1035 [UNPROVEN] The Browser on the KV260 draws some pages badly (CSS; owner, 2026-11-24). The aarch64 images carry the in-house engine only, no full web engine; the page and the CSS features it drops were not captured yet | Closes by: a photo or screenshot with the address, then the browser test harness | Row: F-BROW-06

* KI-1036 [BUG] Raspberry Pi 5: k3s does not come up (owner, 2026-11-24: the start took about three minutes and the cluster did not work). The Pi's device tree passes `cgroup_disable=memory` in its default boot arguments, our kernel has `CONFIG_MEMCG=y` but the memory controller stays off, and the generated `cmdline.txt` does not turn it on; kubelet needs it. Workaround: append `cgroup_enable=memory cgroup_memory=1` to the one line of `cmdline.txt` on the card. Confirmed on the board the same day: `k3s.log` said `failed to find memory cgroup (v2)` five times; with the parameters the memory controller is on and the node `iyakki-dev` became Ready | Closes by: the parameters in the generated Pi boot files (`iyakki-usb rpi files` and the board table), then k3s Ready on a Pi 5 | Row: F-RPI-01 (also F-K8S-01)

* KI-1037 [BUG] Raspberry Pi 5: the `dtoverlay=vc4-kms-v3d` line of the generated `config.txt` does nothing, because the boot volume has no `overlays/overlay_map.dtb` to map it to `vc4-kms-v3d-pi5.dtbo`. The HDMI and GPU nodes stay `disabled`, the firmware framebuffer (`bcm2708_fb`) is used, there is no `/dev/dri` and no sound card, so the Radio tile did not appear (owner, 2026-11-24). Workaround used: `dtoverlay=vc4-kms-v3d-pi5` in `config-user.txt` (then both HDMI sound cards and `/dev/dri` appear) Update 2026-11-25: a card whose `config.txt` itself carries `dtoverlay=vc4-kms-v3d-pi5` (D0 tree, Nov 21 root image) booted to the shell, but `vc4-drm` never finished (HVS bound, no HDMI component, "deferred probe pending") and the shell ran headless with a frozen screen, see KI-1041 | Closes by: the generated `config.txt` naming the `-pi5` overlay, verified on the board | Row: F-RPI-01 (also F-RPI-08)
* KI-1038 [BUG] Raspberry Pi 5 sound: with two HDMI sound cards IYAKKI picks card 0 (the unplugged HDMI-A-1) and writes a direct `hw:0,0` S16_LE configuration, so Radio and the players cannot open the output. The monitor was on HDMI-A-2 (card 1), whose only native format is IEC958 and which plays through `plughw:1,0` (checked with `aplay`). The output should be the card of the connected HDMI port, through the plug converter | Closes by: choosing the card by connector status and probing the format, then Radio audible on the Pi | Row: F-SND-01 (also F-RPI-08)
* KI-1039 [BUG] Raspberry Pi 5 modules: the old BusyBox `modprobe` in the card's root cannot read the xz-compressed modules of the Pi kernel, so no module was loaded (no webcam driver, no netfilter for the dev firewall, no USB audio, `Firewall not available in this kernel`). Workaround used: the modules were decompressed in the root and `modules.dep` regenerated on the PC (62 modules then loaded); the camera then gave `/dev/video0` and k3s ran | Closes by: the image build decompressing the modules (or a `modprobe` that reads xz) and a fresh base root | Row: F-RPI-04 (also F-RPI-08)
* KI-1040 [LIMIT] Raspberry Pi 5 USB webcam (a Shenzhen Fullhan 4 MP, 1d6c:0103): after a cold start of the Pi the webcam enumerated at 12 Mb/s (USB full speed) although it is a high-speed device (bcdUSB 2.00), and then delivered no frames at all (a plain V4L2 test got 0 frames in 5 s at 640x480 and 320x240, MJPEG and YUYV; the CAMERA tile showed nothing and kept re-probing its formats). Unplugging it and plugging it in again after the boot gave 480 Mb/s and a working picture (2026-11-24). A software port reset, a restart of the USB controller and a USB device reset did not change the speed; only cutting the device's power does | Closes by: plugging the webcam in after the boot, or a powered hub; whether the CAMERA tile should explain a full-speed link instead of staying blank is open | Row: F-CAM-06
* KI-1041 [BUG] Raspberry Pi 5 (16 GB, Rev 1.1, D0): with `dtoverlay=vc4-kms-v3d-pi5` in the generated `config.txt` (D0 device tree, overlay file from the Nov 21 build) the kernel binds the HVS and the GPU but never an HDMI component; `vc4-drm` stays in `deferred probe pending (reason unknown)`, `/dev/fb0` never appears, the shell logs `no framebuffer appeared; running headless` at 35 s and the monitor stays on the last kernel console lines (owner, 2026-11-25; the log is `stage1-kmsg.log` on the data volume). With the overlay line commented out the same card booted to the login screen on the firmware frame buffer. An earlier run on 2026-11-24 with the same overlay in `config-user.txt` showed `/dev/dri` and the HDMI sound cards (KI-1037): what differs (line place, monitor port, overlay build) is not found | Closes by: the display on the Pi with the GPU driver and sound, verified on the board | Row: F-RPI-01 (also F-RPI-08)
* KI-1042 [LIMIT] Raspberry Pi 5 menu route (firmware, U-Boot, IYAKKI loader): on the same D0 board the loader never started with Debian U-Boot 2025.01, U-Boot 2026.10-rc5 (Debian experimental) or the community UEFI firmware v0.3 (`worproject/rpi5-uefi`, not updated since March 2024); no `last-boot.log` on the card each time and no screen; no serial adapter was available, so the stop point is not known. The loader and its card format work on the KV260 (F-BOOT-46). The Pi 5 therefore boots directly from the firmware (no menu) | Closes by: a serial log of the Pi's U-Boot, or a D0-capable U-Boot or UEFI build proven on the board | Row: F-BOOT-45

## Browser and media

Format of every entry: `KI-nnn [CLASS] statement | Closes by: ... | Row: ...`. BUG = real defect or missing part, closes by code; UNPROVEN = written and tested with fakes, closes by a named hardware, browser or service session (docs/development/TEST-PROOF-QUEUE.md, docs/development/HARDWARE-TEST-SHEET.md); LIMIT = intended limit or decision.

* KI-017 [LIMIT] H.264 and AAC video does not play by default (patent policy, nothing shipped); YouTube plays VP9 + Opus, TikTok needs the audio and video add-on the person installs (KI-070); playback is software-rendered | Closes by: stays a policy; hardware decoding through VA-API is a later idea | Row: F-BROW-100
* KI-018 [UNPROVEN] The media player on `ffmpeg` (downloaded at the person's request, never shipped) was tested with fakes and a real file decode, never confirmed on a screen | Closes by: a hardware session playing a file in an image without a window system | Row: F-MEDIA-08
* KI-019 [LIMIT] The built-in reader shows Chinese, Japanese, Korean and emoji as boxes (English-first fonts decision) and has no video decoding; video and heavy pages are the full web engine's job (WPE WebKit, large web and macweb images only, tried on Google and YouTube, not across many sites) | Closes by: CJK and emoji fonts are item 1 of docs/BROWSER-ROADMAP.md | Row: F-BROW-26
* KI-020 [BUG] YouTube video and big pages are slow in the full engine on Macs (measured: 60 fps 720p gave 6 to 8 pictures a second, 61 to 80 % dropped; Settings -> Browser -> Smooth pages gave 27 and no drops, but is off by default); Mesa has no Intel driver in the web images so WPE composites on the CPU, one web-process thread uses a whole core while video plays because the T2 audio device runs with a one-frame period, and the full engine's libraries still lack Brotli and WOFF2 (the in-house decoders serve the reader only) | Closes by: the Intel driver (`WEB_IRIS=1`, now a step of the release-green image recipe) in a built image and re-measured on a Mac; the own mixer for T2 audio; details in docs/BROWSER-SPEED.md | Row: F-BROW-59
* KI-021 [UNPROVEN] Ad and tracker blocking: the full engine's rule loading was never run against a real WPE WebKit (helper C not compiled against its headers, no count of blocked requests); about a fifth of the real lists' path rules do not fit WebKit's limit; YouTube video ads are not promised to go (docs/CONTENT-BLOCKING.md); lists are other people's work, downloaded by the person | Closes by: a Mac session with the helper built against real WPE headers | Row: F-BROW-55
* KI-022 [UNPROVEN] Radio: MP3 streams and HLS with MP3 segments play with in-house code; AAC needs the user-downloaded audio add-on and its decoder path is tested with a fake decoder only; Opus has an in-house decoder proven on fixtures, no live station; a station directory search is not built | Closes by: a hardware session with live stations and a real ffmpeg add-on | Row: F-RADIO-03 (also F-RADIO-05, F-RADIO-06, F-RADIO-15)
* KI-096 [UNPROVEN] The committed client engine module was older than its source, so the camera list of the browser client never worked; the module now contains `camera_list` (rebuilt since, last commit touching it e997d4e3) but the Chrome run of `dist/browsertest/run_pages.py` (section client-media) has not been repeated, so the `known="KI-096"` marker in `dist/browsertest/clientsuite.py` stays | Closes by: that Chrome run passing, then remove the marker | Row: F-CLIENT-05
* KI-660 [UNPROVEN] The engine's own response headers (`send_response_headers`, message 0x93 in `dist/webview/wpe-helper/helper.c`) are written and tested through the protocol with fakes; the C was never compiled and no real engine has sent the message (until then the tool sends its own request) | Closes by: a Mac session with the helper built against real WPE headers | Row: F-BROW-75
* KI-661 [UNPROVEN] RTSP over UDP (a camera answering `461` to TCP) and the media server's subscriber list were tried on loopback only; cameras needing `https` or `rtsps` are not supported until the self-signed camera certificate decision is made | Closes by: a hardware session with a real camera, and a TV, VLC or phone app subscribing | Row: F-CAM-15 (also F-MEDIA-02)
* KI-663 [UNPROVEN] A changed custom-header rule now empties the reader's cache and sends `ClearCache` (0x17) to the full engine (`browser_full.rs`, `helper.c clear_cache`); the helper C is not compiled against real WPE, so a page the engine already holds may still need Ctrl+R | Closes by: the same Mac session as KI-660 | Row: F-BROW-79
* KI-711 [UNPROVEN] The decoded-picture and parsed-style caches and Happy Eyeballs are tested but not measured on a real page and a real network with a dead IPv6 route | Closes by: a browser session with timings (queue row) | Row: F-BROX-11 (also F-BROX-13, F-BROX-14)
* KI-712 [UNPROVEN] HTTP/2 (ALPN, one connection per host, fallback to HTTP/1.1) was checked against a server of our own and by hand against three real sites; nobody measured whether pages load faster; server push, HTTP/2 through a proxy, idle keep-alive PINGs and connection sharing between hosts with one certificate are not done | Closes by: a browser session with timings; the missing parts are left out on purpose until measured | Row: F-BROX-10
* KI-713 [UNPROVEN] Safe browsing: the lock-icon view (badge click or Ctrl+U) shows the certificate chain the open tab's own HTTP/2 handshake was given (tested with made-up chains and a fake probe); a site that only speaks HTTP/1.1 still gets a fresh handshake in the panel, the panel and the certificate reader were never run against a real site from the browser window, no known-bad list ships (by decision D-BADSITES-SOURCE the list is the person's own) and only whole sites are listed, not paths | Closes by: a browser session on a real site that opens the lock panel for an HTTP/2 and an HTTP/1.1 site (queue row); paths in the list are a later decision | Row: F-BROW-98
* KI-714 [BUG] SVG drawing still leaves out: an `image` from a file or web address (data: addresses are drawn since alpha.7), `kernelUnitLength` (a real renderer ignores it too); the items `primitiveUnits`, per-primitive `color-interpolation-filters`, pattern `href` and markers from a style sheet were drawn and compared in lane W3; text is not compared with Chrome (fonts differ) | Closes by: code, each part compared against a real headless Chrome drawing | Row: F-BROX-05
* KI-715 [LIMIT] PDF forms: text, choice, check box and radio button fields can be filled (multi-line text is wrapped at the field width, comb fields put one character in each cell); push buttons and signatures are refused on purpose, JavaScript formatting and calculation are not run, the new appearance uses Helvetica, protected files are not filled, and the copy is always a new file; filled files show their values in Apple PDFKit and poppler, other readers were not tried | Closes by: a decision to build signatures, push-button actions or other fonts (IMPL-REMAINDER.md); a session in other readers | Row: F-BROX-08

* KI-024 [UNPROVEN] Claude Code and GitHub Copilot CLI were installed and signed in on the Mac mini; Copilot with real tool calls, Autopilot with external agents ('Allow Auto') and the Codex and Gemini CLI adapters (fake CLIs only) were not run end to end (docs/EXTERNAL-AGENTS.md) | Closes by: a session on a device with real sign-ins | Row: F-JOBS-04

## Accounts, files and security

* KI-040 [LIMIT] IYAKKI accounts are not Linux users, and user files on the data volume (`/home`) are root-owned (KI-095 changes who can read the home folders, not who owns them) | Closes by: stays by design; a per-person Linux user is not planned | Row: none
* KI-041 [UNPROVEN] The firewall (`docs/FIREWALL.md`: incoming blocked by default, outgoing and forwarding untouched, host-name rules only as trustworthy as the DNS server) was tested with fakes and a real kernel in a network namespace, never on a device with k3s running; a kernel without nf_tables says so and is not protected | Closes by: a hardware session on a Mac mini with k3s up (Keep/Revert, presets, a blocked probe) | Row: F-FW-04
* KI-042 [LIMIT] The root filesystem is in RAM: the layered root (base, core, ui layers) keeps its layer files on the boot or data volume but the writable layer above them is RAM-backed, so changes outside `/var/lib/iyakki` and `/home` vanish at reboot (`docs/development/LAYERED-ROOT-DESIGN.md`) | Closes by: stays by design | Row: F-BOOT-41
* KI-043 [LIMIT] The data volume as a whole is not encrypted (an encrypted home folder per account exists, KI-001), and the browser front end uses a self-signed certificate that has had no outside review | Closes by: the whole-volume slice of F-STORE-05; the outside review is an owner decision | Row: F-STORE-05
* KI-045 [UNPROVEN] Payments for paid apps: the checkout, token and refund code is written (Impl 100) but no payment provider was ever used and the Buy button stays off without one | Closes by: the owner choosing a provider and one real purchase and refund in a hardware session | Row: F-APPSTORE-13
* KI-046 [LIMIT] The official store root key and hosted catalog do not exist: `official-roots.txt` has only comments and `OFFICIAL_STORE_URL` is empty | Closes by: the owner generating the root key and hosting the catalog (the code and tests are in the tree) | Row: F-APPSTORE-14
* KI-103 [LIMIT] Lines typed into an interactive developer-mode terminal are not recorded by decision (the audit line gives bytes and line count only, no hash, because a hash would let a typed password be guessed; test `devmode::tty_tests`, "lines (not recorded)") | Closes by: stays by design | Row: none
* KI-105 [BUG] In an unencrypted backup file the account name is only a claim, so "made for this account" proves nothing and a planted file could carry skills or settings; the `backup_restore` card names the file and areas and always asks, but content is checked by checksums only, and a passphrase sits in an ordinary string while used (security review 2026-11-12, low) | Closes by: authenticate unencrypted backups with a per-account key from the vault and hold the passphrase in a wiped buffer | Row: F-BACKUP-01
* KI-130 [UNPROVEN] The pages refreshed by the events stream (`app.js` `poll(..., {push: true})`, `bell.js`, `cloud.js`, `remote.js`) were written on a Mac with no `node` and no Chrome and never executed; the server side is tested with a real Rust WebSocket client over TLS | Closes by: running the node tests and `dist/browsertest/run_pages.py` in a real Chrome session | Row: F-WEB-16
* KI-132 [LIMIT] A host process shows connections, listening ports and UDP sockets only; received and sent bytes exist only for a process in its own network namespace (a container) because the kernel counts bytes per namespace | Closes by: stays until an eBPF or conntrack accounting design is accepted | Row: F-SYS-01
* KI-133 [UNPROVEN] A script run with input (`IYAKKI_STDIN` through a `sh` wrapper) and the real stop were tested only against the stand-in cluster, and the Input bar was not looked at on a screen | Closes by: a hardware session with a real k3s and the editor on a screen | Row: F-EDIT-16
* KI-150 [UNPROVEN] The download list changes the version of thirteen tools, but the download and checksum addresses still come from the program's templates, nothing is published at the address yet (no real fetch was ever made), `cli_download_list_check` reaches the network so no test runs it, and the release gate's manifest step does not check the signature | Closes by: the owner publishing a signed list, then one real check on a device and a signature step in the gate | Row: F-QA-11
* KI-151 [UNPROVEN] The Add source form fills its address late, when a slow cluster finally answers; tested with a slow fake cluster on a Mac, not on a screen and not against a real k3s | Closes by: a hardware session with a real k3s | Row: F-DASH-12
* KI-152 [UNPROVEN] Tenant configuration secrets are written, read and removed only when `IYAKKI_VAULT_FINGERPRINT` is set; tested against a loopback vault service only, not in a real pod | Closes by: a run in a real tenant pod on a cluster | Row: F-CLOUD-33

* KI-051 [LIMIT] Native web views are not all built: Chat (85) and Settings (70) tiles are below Impl 100, and the Tiles page and the docked IDE preview are separate; the editor does compile to WebAssembly (F-WEB-05) | Closes by: F-WEB-53 and F-WEB-56 reaching Impl 100 | Row: F-WEB-04, F-WEB-53, F-WEB-56

## SSH tunnels

* KI-111 [UNPROVEN] SSH tunnels were only tested with a fake client (docs/SSH-TUNNELS.md, "What is verified"); that OpenSSH 10.2 accepts the exact arguments and that its error texts match the mapping is unproven | Closes by: one session against a real SSH host | Row: F-SSHTUN-01
* KI-112 [UNPROVEN] `-J` (jump hosts) may fail on the images: OpenSSH starts the jump hop with its compiled-in `ssh` path (`/usr/bin/ssh`), which is Dropbear's client there, while the git-ssh package installs OpenSSH only as `/usr/libexec/iyakki/git-ssh` (the recipe now sets it, but no image was built with it yet); a single host is not affected | Closes by: the package now sets `_PATH_SSH_PROGRAM` to `/usr/libexec/iyakki/git-ssh` (post-patch hook in `dist/buildroot/package/iyakki-git-ssh/iyakki-git-ssh.mk`, build stops if the source line is not found); build the next image with it, then open a tunnel with a jump chain (`-J`) from a session on a device and see the jump hop connect | Row: F-SSHTUN-01
* KI-113 [LIMIT] Per-site routing is not built: the Browser does not pick a tunnel by domain (seam `sshtunnel::SiteRouter`); open the address shown on the page | Closes by: a new feature row (none exists yet) | Row: none
* KI-114 [LIMIT] A host that git also uses loses its trusted-host line when the last tunnel using it is deleted; git then shows the fingerprint again | Closes by: stays; harmless | Row: F-SSHTUN-01
* KI-115 [LIMIT] IPv6 literal host names are not accepted in a tunnel; use a name | Closes by: stays until asked for | Row: F-SSHTUN-01

* KI-055 [UNPROVEN] zram was seen in QEMU only, behaviour under real memory pressure is untested; there is no hibernation (by design) | Closes by: a hardware session that fills memory on a real machine | Row: F-ZRAM-01
* KI-056 [UNPROVEN] The release signing key and download page are new: every release carries `SHA256SUMS` and a signed `release.json`; the printed public key must be compared with the README by the person | Closes by: the alpha.7 release check performing that comparison on the published files | Row: none
* KI-057 [UNPROVEN] An image made before the version bump says `unknown` for the version in About; the stamp `/etc/iyakki-release` has not been seen on a built image since the change | Closes by: reading About on a fresh image (hardware or QEMU session) | Row: F-REL-01

## Licence notes

Licence notes are statements, not issues, and live in [LICENSING.md](LICENSING.md) and [docs/LICENSES.md](docs/LICENSES.md) (GPL source offer for the images: LICENSING.md item 4 and docs/LICENSES.md; third-party tools downloaded at the person's click and never shipped: LICENSING.md items 5 and 10; restricted firmware left out of the images: LICENSING.md item 7 and [docs/RESTRICTED-FIRMWARE.md](docs/RESTRICTED-FIRMWARE.md)). The former KI-065 and KI-066 are those statements.

## Entries not yet sorted into a section

_These entries stood under "Licence notes" by an old accident; they are not licence statements. They are to be rewritten into the one-line form and moved into the right section._

* KI-069 [UNPROVEN] Browser microphone and WebAudio on the Mac: the page freeze (output that could not negotiate) is fixed in code by the shared `iyakkiwebaudiosink` (`dist/webview/wpe-helper/websink.c`: one device handle, one mixer thread, silence when the device is unusable) and was confirmed on the Mac mini with hot patches (2026-10-28), but the image build was never re-run through a microphone page; open: after a write error the stream stays silent until its next start, and two engine processes (Browser tile and the AI IDE browser) exclude each other on the single-use T2 speaker and microphone | Closes by: TEST-PROOF-QUEUE row F-BROW-77 on an Apple machine (a recorder page and a WebAudio page), then the mixer/dsnoop sharing | Row: F-BROW-77
* KI-070 [UNPROVEN] MP3 decodes in the engine through `iyakkimp3dec` (boot self-test `path mp3-decoder`), AAC and H.264 need the administrator's FFmpeg add-on (IYAKKI ships none, owner decision 2026-11-04); the bridge plugin is in an image only after `./build.sh web`, the Settings -> Browser add-on mirror page is not built, `media.codecs` is only documented, and nothing is confirmed on real hardware | Closes by: a web-engine rebuild with the bridge, then TEST-PROOF-QUEUE row F-BROW-72 and a Mac mini session playing MP3, AAC and H.264 pages (docs/development/AV-ADDON-BUILD.md) | Row: F-BROW-72
* KI-071 [LIMIT] The images carry neither podman nor docker, so `deploy_build` fails and the IDE assistant must not apply an image it never built (the skills `k3s-node-webapp` and `k3s-web-service-patterns` carry a no-builder rule: stop, or use a stock pinned image with the files in a ConfigMap); shipping podman is an owner decision not yet recorded | Closes by: stays until the owner decides on podman (size not measured) | Row: none
* KI-072 [UNPROVEN] No automatic sign-in exists in the tree (tests `shell::nosignin_tests`, a scan of the program and image scripts, `vaultpending` waits at once on a locked vault, `settings::vaultstate_tests`), but a Mac was once seen with the vault locked after boot and that cause was never proven | Closes by: a Mac mini session: sign in with the password, install Gitea from Store -> Setup, confirm no "locked" appears; if it does, `iyakki dev diag` | Row: F-SHELL-01
* KI-073 [BUG] Sleep on Apple T2 MacBooks wakes badly (image 1028u): the wake takes long, Wi-Fi does not come back and the keyboard does not work at the sign-in screen (apple-bce and the Wi-Fi card do not resume); treat Sleep as experimental there | Closes by: a suspend hook that unloads and reloads apple-bce and the Wi-Fi driver around suspend, the kernel log of a wake, and hiding Sleep on T2 until it resumes cleanly; then a wake test on a T2 MacBook Pro (TEST-PROOF-QUEUE row F-KBD-16); the F-KBD-16 note "kernel with suspend not built" and the 1028u try disagree and need one honest wording | Row: F-KBD-16
* KI-074 [UNPROVEN] LEARN lessons (F-STUDY-05, F-STUDY-06) were tested only with scripted models: no real model wrote a lesson, followed the five-section format, drew an SVG or a raster picture, and read-aloud and the page were never seen on a real speaker or screen (a lesson is not in the backup, the tile uses the chat model); the owner accepted plans and lessons on the Mac 2026-10-30 | Closes by: a live run against a real model server judged by a person (docs/development/TEST-GAPS-M27.md) | Row: F-STUDY-05
* KI-075 [BUG] The Linux windows (Debian sandbox) install fails at the apt step on the Mac (image 1028u): download and unpack work, the reason was not captured, and a full apt run inside the sandbox on an image never completed (the `pivot_root` fix is proven in QEMU only) | Closes by: read the Installs row's Log on the Mac mini (or run `iyakki linuxapp-init` by hand through `iyakki dev shell`), fix the cause, rerun | Row: F-LINAPP-03
* KI-076 [UNPROVEN] VPN was never brought up on hardware: the WireGuard module is in the image but no tunnel was run against a real server and the OpenVPN download and helper were not exercised (the Lenovo and Mac mini 2018 only ran the read-only F-VPN-07 recipe) | Closes by: a hardware session: bring a WireGuard tunnel up from the VPN page against a real server and read the kill-switch line on the Firewall page | Row: F-VPN-11 (to F-VPN-16)

* KI-994 [UNPROVEN] The boot counter does not step with the BIOS GRUB of an install image repacked from a kernel donor built before 2026-11-14 (`grubenv` counter stays 1, the menu says `can't find command load_env`): `repack.sh` copied the donor's GRUB, which lacked `loadenv`. Cause found by Q6 (docs/development/UPDATE-FAULTS.md); `repack.sh` now makes the GRUB images again from the defconfig's lists (`dist/buildroot/grub-refresh.sh`, test `grub_refresh`), and with such a core the five update-fault cases pass in QEMU. Not yet seen: an image built after this change, run through `dist/qemu/boot-counter-test.sh` on its own BIOS GRUB; the signed (Secure Boot) chain still takes its list from the tree's own `.config` | Closes by: building or repacking an install image now and running `dist/qemu/boot-counter-test.sh` and `dist/qemu/update-faults-test.py` on it as built (no `--grub-core`) | Row: F-UPD-02

* KI-1000 [UNPROVEN] Quick install of k3s left the job running because the start guard matched any process named k3s; the guard now counts only a real server or agent (test `iyakki-agent::k3sdev::tests::the_process_guard_matches_a_real_server_not_a_helper_or_our_own_child`) but was not re-seen in a guest | Closes by: a QEMU session on a current image running `iyakki quick-install k3s` to the end | Row: none
* KI-1013 [BUG] Lenovo Legion Pro 5 (16ARX8): the kernel registers no backlight (`/sys/class/backlight` empty, only keyboard lock LEDs), so the Display page offers no brightness; the missing part is not proven (AMD/NVIDIA backlight option, a platform driver or lockdown) | Closes by: reading the boot lines of the kmsg file of a fresh boot, then enabling the right kernel option or driver | Row: F-KBD-03
* KI-1023 [LIMIT] When a Bluetooth headset disconnects, the radio and the music it plays now pause; the external-player queue and the video player tile are not registered as pausers yet, and the setting to move the sound to the speakers is parked (audit P-05) | Closes by: registering those players with `BtRoute::on_lost` and a headset leaving on real hardware | Row: F-LIFE-14
* KI-1028 [UNPROVEN] Lifecycle behaviour not measured: players and capture after a real suspend, radio output after a sound card loss, sandboxes and ptys after a core crash, device-key skew and firewall expiry on a wrong server clock; the small items written and unit-tested in alpha.10 (a USB drive pulled out while open, NTP after the link returns, SSH back-off reset, a VPN roam with no uplink, an idle limit on the chat stream) still wait for a run on real hardware (audit P-04, P-15 to P-17; fixed in code: P-08, P-10 to P-13) | Closes by: runs on real hardware (Manual tests checklists) and a policy for the clock skew | Row: F-LIFE-18, F-LIFE-19
